10. Debt-funded revenue
11. DFRI publishes two linked records. It estimates the share of covered companies' U.S. consumer revenue funded by new consumer credit. It also predicts each month's change in U.S. consumer borrowing before the Federal Reserve publishes the official G.19 figure, records that forecast with a timestamp, and grades it against the first print.

24. Across the same publication, covered-company midpoint estimates range from 1.45% at Booking Holdings to 18.67% at Carvana. The 3.36% headline is revenue-weighted, not a threshold or a typical-company score.
25. Midpoint represents an estimated $19,792 million of debt-funded consumer revenue over an estimated $588,888 million denominator. Bands are always shown.

28. The naive comparator is refit for each target using only first prints available when that prediction was recorded. These statistics update from live grades only and remain separate from the historical backtest.
29. First revolving miss
30. The prediction forecast -5,529 $M; the first print was +6,800 $M. It missed the sign and fell outside its 80% interval. Its absolute error was about 12,330 $M, versus a 4,633 $M 101-month backtest MAE.
31. One miss in one graded pair is statistically uninformative, but DFRI flags it rather than burying it. The model has not been retrained or retuned in response.

9. Local preview. Not a public live-cycle record. 0 pre-public engineering prediction(s) excluded.
10. Methodology
11. The nowcast estimates monthly G.19 credit flows. Attribution then applies registered product-to-category weights (Matrix A) and category-to-company weights (Matrix B), divided by estimated U.S. consumer revenue. Every judgmental uncertain input has a public assumption ID and band; fixed derived mappings retain their method and evidence.
12. Primary nowcast sources: Federal Reserve Board H.8 release archive, Census MARTS historic releases, and the Federal Reserve Board G.19 release archive used for first-print grading.
13. Read the complete code-derived modeling architecture, including implemented benchmarks, selection rules, point-in-time controls, attribution mechanics, and documented divergences.
14. Methodology 1.1.1 preserves the 50-company coverage boundary and corrects Carvana's auto-market and originated-finance classification. Compare 1.1.0 with 1.1.1 or inspect the dated coverage and exclusion ledger.
15. Bridge model
16. Partial H.8 months expose observed coverage explicitly. Missing retail data remains missing and is represented by an explicit availability indicator whose coefficient is fitted with the bridge.
17. First-print grading
18. The dated release page supplies the target-month preliminary level and revised prior-month level used for the release-coherent flow.
19. Ledger
20. Predictions and grades are separate. Model upgrades create new versions; old rows are neither restated nor deleted.
21. Tier 1
22. Observed: a disclosure directly links financing to the company's sales. The company page links the filing and a quote of no more than 15 words.
23. Tier 2
24. Category-mapped: credit flow is allocated to spend categories and then to companies by estimated category revenue share.
25. Tier 3
26. Fungible: unassignable flows are allocated in proportion to covered consumer revenue, with the widest bands.
27. Credit flow
28. The view uses the registered current-period midpoint flow, Matrix A midpoint, and Matrix B midpoint for each lane. It groups only after those published mappings are applied, keeps every remainder group within one evidence tier, and does not enter the nowcast or change any company estimate.

64. Nonrevolving Other Tier 2 TIER 2 $3,240M CVNA $221M; GM $1,232M; All other $1,787M
65. Evidence Lift
66. Evidence Lift equals a company's estimated DFR% midpoint divided by its pure Tier 3 fungibility baseline for the same period. The baseline is a counterfactual built from the already-computed broad proportional lanes—general retail plus the two fungible categories—with company-specific financing and auto-category evidence removed.
67. A value of 1.00× is baseline-only: no company-specific financing evidence was found, so the estimate reflects proportional allocation. A higher multiple shows how much registered evidence lifts the midpoint above that baseline. It does not measure risk, credit quality, or investment merit.
68. Backtest coverage
69. In the point-in-time backtest, the nominal 80% band contained 71.3% of first prints and the nominal 95% band contained 93.1%. Both currently under-cover their nominal rates, especially the 80% interval, so these bands are empirical uncertainty ranges, not guarantees.
70. Assumptions
71. Low / midpoint / high values are triangular priors unless all three values are identical. Source files are public so a cold clone can reproduce every result.
72. Every non-observed attribution parameter in methodology 1.1.1.

215. The annual filing supplies geographic and product-category revenue; U.S. direct and wholesale consumer pass-through receives a wide band. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
216. A-DEN-PEP-001 TIER 2 PepsiCo U.S. consumer revenue is 35% to 74% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.35 / 0.55 / 0.74 Source
217. The annual filing supplies geographic food-and-beverage revenue; U.S. consumer sell-through is modeled from manufacturer revenue. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
218. A-DEN-PG-001 TIER 2 Procter & Gamble U.S. consumer revenue is 22% to 64% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.22 / 0.42 / 0.64 Source
219. The annual filing supplies geographic consumer-products revenue; U.S. consumer sell-through is modeled from manufacturer revenue. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
220. A-DEN-TPR-001 TIER 2 Tapestry U.S. consumer revenue is 25% to 72% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.25 / 0.48 / 0.72 Source
221. The annual filing supplies geographic and direct-to-consumer channel revenue; U.S. consumer revenue remains modeled. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
222. A-T1-CVNA-FINANCE-001 TIER 1 Carvana receives 1% to 3% of national nonrevolving flow through its originated used-auto finance channel. 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.03 Source
223. Since February 2013, Carvana has also offered and originated loans to consumers. The wide prior is scaled below GM and Ford using Carvana's smaller registered consumer-revenue denominator; the filing and ABS trust establish origination.
224. Matrix A and B
225. Matrix A has 15 product-to-category rows. Matrix B has 165 category-to-company rows. Tests reject negative weights, missing evidence, unregistered uncertainty, or row allocations over 100%.

10. Coverage
11. The P1 universe partitions every Consumer Discretionary and Consumer Staples member in the pinned, cross-checked S&P 500 snapshot. Historical coverage is preserved by methodology version; no past company set is rewritten.
12. Verified 2026-08-05. Fifty included, 31 excluded, with a one-line reason for every exclusion. Retain the ten P0 issuers, then rank passing issuers by direct consumer-revenue evidence, geographic evidence, filing completeness, and ticker as the deterministic tie-break. No price, market-cap, or vendor data is used.

16. 2026-08-09 · publication · 1.1.1

19. Companies · Prediction ledger · Evidence Lift
20. 2026-08-08 · publication · 1.1.1

23. Credit flow · Methodology
24. 2026-08-08 · publication · bridge-ridge-v2-alpha10
25. First live grades published without softening

27. Scoreboard · Scoreboard feed

35. Evidence Lift · Carvana · Company feed v2 · Feed schema v2
